Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me today, I really appreciate your time. 

Um... I hope it's okay to just jump right in, but I guess what brings me in is that I keep getting hit out of nowhere with... well, with the scene of my cousin on that pavement. I'm sorry, I know that sounds a bit dramatic, and I really don't mean to make a huge deal out of it. It's just... it's like I can hear all the shouting and see the lights all over again.

Um... yeah, I guess I do, a little bit. I mean, I really try not to let it affect my daily routine too much, because people depend on me, you know? But... well, I do tend to take the long way around town now. Just so I don't have to pass by the community center... or that basketball court. And, um, if people at church or in the neighborhood start talking about that night... I usually try to gently change the subject, or I just remember something I have to go do in the office and excuse myself. I don't want to be rude to anyone, I just... I get kind of overwhelmed. Is that a normal thing to do? I hope I don't sound like I'm just running away from my problems.

Um... yeah, I do. I mean, that's actually the main reason I finally decided to come in and talk to someone. I... I didn't want to make a big deal out of it, because I know people go through worse, but...

It's just... it's like I can hear all the shouting and see the lights all over again. \n\nAnd it happens at the worst times, like even when I'm just standing in the church office trying to act normal and do my job. I really do try to keep it together and not let it show, I promise. Does... does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm explaining things the way you need me to.\n\nDoctor: When did the incident with your cousin happen?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, it was two years and three months ago. That's when he was... when he was killed. And I guess this has all been going on ever since then. \n\nI know that's a really long time, and I'm so sorry, I feel like I probably should have moved past it by now, especially given my role at the church. I really have tried to just put it behind me and be strong for everyone else, I promise. I hope it's okay that I'm still struggling with it after all this time... am I answering your question alright?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain places, people, or activities because they remind you of your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess I do, a little bit. I mean, I really try not to let it affect my daily routine too much, because people depend on me, you know? But... well, I do tend to take the long way around town now. Just so I don't have to pass by the community center... or that basketball court. It's just a small detour, really, it doesn't take that much extra time. \n\nAnd, um, if people at church or in the neighborhood start talking about that night... I usually try to gently change the subject, or I just remember something I have to go do in the office and excuse myself. I don't want to be rude to anyone, I just... I get kind of overwhelmed. Is that a normal thing to do? I hope I don't sound like I'm just running away from my problems.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sleeping at night?\n\nPatient: Um... no, actually, I sleep pretty well. I mean, I try to make sure I keep myself really busy during the day—just helping out the congregation and doing all my duties—so by the time I get to bed, I'm usually exhausted enough to just sleep right through. \n\nI hope that's okay? But yeah, my sleep is mostly fine, thankfully. Does... does that help?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel on edge or easily irritated?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try hard to be patient and understanding with everyone. That's a big part of my calling, you know? Being a source of peace for the congregation. But... I guess, lately, I have been a little... on edge. \n\nI'm so embarrassed to even admit this, but... there have been a few times in ministry meetings where I've just... snapped. Over really small things. I actually raised my voice at some of the deacons, and even the youth leaders, almost like... like they were against me or something. \n\nI always feel terrible right after, I really do. I hate how fast I get angry like that, and I always make sure to apologize. I know I shouldn't be acting that way, especially as a pastor. I hope you don't think I'm a bad person for it... I'm really trying to work on it. Is it normal to feel so easily frustrated like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're reliving the moment of your cousin's death, like you're right back there?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I do. Um... I know I shouldn't. I really try not to. As a pastor, I'm always teaching my congregation about grace and trusting in God, so I feel terrible even admitting this to you. I don't want you to think I don't have faith, or that I'm not trying my best to cope, but... yes. I do. 

I just... I keep telling myself that if I had only ended the church event a little earlier... or if I had just taken the time to walk him out... or even if I had just kept a better watch over those boys who were hanging around... my cousin would still be here. 

I know it's probably not right for me to take all that on, and I'm sorry if it sounds like I'm just wallowing in it. I know I shouldn't dwell on the "what ifs." I really am trying to be strong and move past it. Do you... do you think it's normal to blame yourself like that? I just hope I'm not making your evaluation harder by being so stuck on this.

Is that a normal thing to do? I hope I don't sound like I'm just running away from my problems.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sleeping at night?\n\nPatient: Um... no, actually, I sleep pretty well. I mean, I try to make sure I keep myself really busy during the day—just helping out the congregation and doing all my duties—so by the time I get to bed, I'm usually exhausted enough to just sleep right through. \n\nI hope that's okay? I know you're probably trying to figure out a specific pattern, and I really don't want to mess up your evaluation or be difficult by not having that problem. But yeah, my sleep is mostly fine, thankfully. Does... does that help?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel on edge or easily irritated?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try hard to be patient and understanding with everyone. That's a big part of my calling, you know? Being a source of peace for the congregation. But... I guess, lately, I have been a little... on edge. \n\nI'm so embarrassed to even admit this, but... there have been a few times in ministry meetings where I've just... snapped. Over really small things. I actually raised my voice at some of the deacons, and even the youth leaders, almost like... like they were against me or something. \n\nI always feel terrible right after, I really do. I hate how fast I get angry like that, and I always make sure to apologize. I know I shouldn't be acting that way, especially as a pastor. I hope you don't think I'm a bad person for it... I'm really trying to work on it. Is it normal to feel so easily frustrated like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're reliving the moment of your cousin's death, like you're right back there?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I do. I mean, that's actually the main reason I finally decided to come in and talk to someone. I... I didn't want to make a big deal out of it, because I know people go through worse, but... \n\nIt's just that, out of nowhere, I'll get hit with the whole scene again. Like... like I'm right back there. I can see my cousin on the pavement, and... I can hear all the shouting, and see the flashing lights. And the hardest part is, it happens even when I'm just standing in the church office, trying to act completely normal and be there for everyone else. \n\nI try so hard to just push it down and focus on my work, but it feels so real. I feel awful even bringing it up, because I should be stronger than this, right? I'm supposed to be the one helping people through their grief, not... falling apart in the middle of the day. I hope that makes sense? I just want to know if there's a way to make it stop, so I can get back to being the pastor my congregation needs.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Um... I know I shouldn't. I really try not to. As a pastor, I'm always teaching my congregation about grace and trusting in God, so I feel terrible even admitting this to you. I don't want you to think I don't have faith, or that I'm not trying my best to cope, but... yes. I do. \n\nI just... I keep telling myself that if I had only ended the church event a little earlier... or if I had just taken the time to walk him out... or even if I had just kept a better watch over those boys who were hanging around... my cousin would still be here. \n\nI know it's probably not right for me to take all that on, and I'm sorry if it sounds like I'm just wallowing in it. It's just a small detour, really, it doesn't take that much extra time. \n\nAnd, um, if people at church or in the neighborhood start talking about that night... I usually try to gently change the subject, or I just remember something I have to go do in the office and excuse myself. I don't want to be rude to anyone, I just... I get kind of overwhelmed. Is that a normal thing to do? I hope I don't sound like I'm just running away from my problems.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sleeping at night?\n\nPatient: Um... no, actually, I sleep pretty well. I mean, I try to make sure I keep myself really busy during the day—just helping out the congregation and doing all my duties—so by the time I get to bed, I'm usually exhausted enough to just sleep right through. \n\nI hope that's okay? I know you're probably trying to figure out a specific pattern, and I really don't want to mess up your evaluation or be difficult by not having that problem. But yeah, my sleep is mostly fine, thankfully. Does... does that help?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel on edge or easily irritated?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try hard to be patient and understanding with everyone. That's a big part of my calling, you know? Being a source of peace for the congregation. But... I guess, lately, I have been a little... on edge. \n\nI'm so embarrassed to even admit this, but... there have been a few times in ministry meetings where I've just... snapped. Over really small things. I actually raised my voice at some of the deacons, and even the youth leaders, almost like... like they were against me or something. \n\nI always feel terrible right after, I really do. I hate how fast I get angry like that, and I always make sure to apologize. I know I shouldn't be acting that way, especially as a pastor. I hope you don't think I'm a bad person for it... I'm really trying to work on it. Is it normal to feel so easily frustrated like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're reliving the moment of your cousin's death, like you're right back there?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I do. I mean, that's actually the main reason I finally decided to come in and talk to someone. I... I didn't want to make a big deal out of it, because I know people go through worse, but... \n\nIt's just that, out of nowhere, I'll get hit with the whole scene again. Like... like I'm right back there. I can see my cousin on the pavement, and... I can hear all the shouting, and see the flashing lights. And the hardest part is, it happens even when I'm just standing in the church office, trying to act completely normal and be there for everyone else. \n\nI try so hard to just push it down and focus on my work, but it feels so real. I feel awful even bringing it up, because I should be stronger than this, right? I'm supposed to be the one helping people through their grief, not... falling apart in the middle of the day. I hope that makes sense? I just want to know if there's a way to make it stop, so I can get back to being the pastor my congregation needs.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Um... I know I shouldn't. I really try not to. As a pastor, I'm always teaching my congregation about grace and trusting in God, so I feel terrible even admitting this to you. I don't want you to think I don't have faith, or that I'm not trying my best to cope, but... yes. I do. \n\nI just... I keep telling myself that if I had only ended the church event a little earlier... or if I had just taken the time to walk him out... or even if I had just kept a better watch over those boys who were hanging around... my cousin would still be here. \n\nI know it's probably not right for me to take all that on, and I'm sorry if it sounds like I'm just wallowing in it. I know I shouldn't dwell on the \"what ifs.\" I really am trying to be strong and move past it. Do you... do you think it's normal to blame yourself like that? I just hope I'm not making your evaluation harder by being so stuck on this.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body watching yourself when these memories of your cousin's death come back?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think I've ever felt quite like that. I hope that's okay—I mean, I want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need. But when it happens, it's really just... I'm right back in my own shoes, feeling everything all over again. \n\nI don't really feel disconnected or like I'm floating above it or watching it happen to someone else. I'm just... trapped right there in the middle of it. I'm sorry if that's not the typical response, or if I'm not explaining it right. Is it... is it normal that I don't have that happen to me? Um... well, I really try to just focus on the positive, you know? I want to be a good example for the congregation, so I... I try not to dwell on the negative feelings too much. I hope that doesn't make it sound like I'm in denial or anything. I just... I want to handle it the right way.

Um... well, I really try to stay engaged, you know? I want to be present for my congregation. They rely on me so much, and I always try to put on a brave face for them. I hope you don't think I'm ignoring my duties or anything like that. 

But... if I'm being completely honest... yes, sometimes I do feel a bit... disconnected. It's hard to explain without sounding like I'm ungrateful for my community. Even when I'm surrounded by people at church, and they're calling me 'brother' and asking me to pray with them... I just feel like... like I'm behind glass. Like they're right there, but nobody can really reach me. 

I feel so guilty about it, because I know they love me and they mean well. I shouldn't feel so distant from them. I'm sorry, I know a pastor shouldn't feel that way about his flock. Do you... do you think that makes me a bad leader? I just want to make sure I'm not failing them by feeling this way.

I really just want to make sure I'm being helpful and answering your questions the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid talking or thinking about your feelings related to your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to just focus on the positive, you know? I want to handle it the right way.\n\nBut, um... yeah, I guess I do avoid it. I actually... I take the long way around town now, just so I don't have to drive past the community center or the basketball court where it happened. And... if someone brings up that night, even if they mean well, I usually try to change the subject or... or I'll just find an excuse to leave the room. \n\nI know I should probably be more open to talking about it, especially since I encourage others to share their burdens. I just want to make sure I'm doing what I need to do to get better.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel numb or disconnected from the world around you when you're not having one of these flashbacks?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to stay engaged, you know? Um... well, I really try to keep up with everything. I don't want to let anyone down, so I still show up and do what's expected of me. I hope you know I'm still trying my best... I don't want to sound lazy or ungrateful for the life and the calling God has given me.

I didn't want to make a big deal out of it, because I know people go through worse, but... \n\nIt's just that, out of nowhere, I'll get hit with the whole scene again. Like... like I'm right back there. I can see my cousin on the pavement, and... I can hear all the shouting, and see the flashing lights. I feel awful even bringing it up, because I should be stronger than this, right? I'm supposed to be the one helping people through their grief, not... falling apart in the middle of the day. I hope that makes sense? I just want to know if there's a way to make it stop, so I can get back to being the pastor my congregation needs.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Um... I know I shouldn't. I really try not to. As a pastor, I'm always teaching my congregation about grace and trusting in God, so I feel terrible even admitting this to you. I don't want you to think I don't have faith, or that I'm not trying my best to cope, but... yes. I do. \n\nI just... I keep telling myself that if I had only ended the church event a little earlier... or if I had just taken the time to walk him out... or even if I had just kept a better watch over those boys who were hanging around... my cousin would still be here. \n\nI know it's probably not right for me to take all that on, and I'm sorry if it sounds like I'm just wallowing in it. I just feel like... like I'm behind glass. Like they're right there, but nobody can really reach me. \n\nI feel so guilty about it, because I know they love me and they mean well. I shouldn't feel so distant from them. I'm sorry, I know a pastor shouldn't feel that way about his flock. Do you... do you think that makes me a bad leader? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, I really try to maintain good relationships, you know? I want everyone at the church to feel supported and loved. I don't want you to think I'm just abandoning my responsibilities or fighting with people on purpose. I really do try to keep the peace.

But, um... things have been a little rocky lately. I... I actually was put on temporary leave from ministry. It wasn't... I mean, I had a bit of an outburst during a church-board meeting. I just got so overwhelmed, and I blew up in a way I never normally would. I feel so ashamed just saying it. And... I've also had to step back from leading the youth events. The parents and the other leaders... they just don't really trust me to stay steady right now, which I completely understand. 

I know a pastor is supposed to be a pillar of strength, and I feel like I've really let everyone down by not keeping my emotions in check. I'm so sorry, I know that makes me sound like a terrible leader. Is it... is it okay that I'm taking this time away? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing so I can heal and get back to serving them the way I should.

Um... well, I really try not to use words like 'worthless' to describe myself. I teach my congregation that we all have God-given value, and I don't want you to think I'm just throwing a pity party or ignoring my faith. I really do try to keep a healthy perspective on things.

I'm sorry, I know I shouldn't be so hard on myself, and I hope you don't think less of me for struggling with this guilt. Do you... do you think it's normal to blame yourself like that? I just want to make sure I'm answering you correctly so I can figure out how to get past this.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you stopped doing things that you used to enjoy since your cousin's death?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 24 / clergy / bachelors\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Loss_of_Interest\nmanifestation: \"I used to love preaching, playing keys, taking pictures, and running with the guys on the court, but now I mostly go through the motions or avoid it altogether because none of it feels alive in me anymore.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, I know we touched on this a little bit already, and I really want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture. I don't want to sound like a broken record or like I'm just complaining, because I really am trying to push through and do what I'm supposed to do. 

But, um... yeah, like I mentioned before... I really have stopped doing a lot of the things I used to love. It's just... I used to get so much joy out of preaching and playing the keys for worship. And I loved taking pictures, and playing basketball with the guys... but since everything happened, I just... I either just go through the motions because I have to, or I avoid those things completely. 

It just feels like the life has been drained out of them, you know? None of it feels alive in me anymore. I feel so bad repeating that, because I know I should be stronger in my faith and find joy in the Lord's work. I'm sorry if I sound ungrateful or like I'm not trying hard enough. Is it... is it helpful for me to explain it that way? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me today, I really appreciate your time. \n\nUm... I'm sorry, I know that sounds a bit dramatic, and I really don't mean to make a huge deal out of it. It's just... it's like I can hear all the shouting and see the lights all over again. \n\nAnd it happens at the worst times, like even when I'm just standing in the church office trying to act normal and do my job. I really do try to keep it together and not let it show, I promise. Does... does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm explaining things the way you need me to.\n\nDoctor: When did the incident with your cousin happen?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, it was two years and three months ago. That's when he was... when he was killed. And I guess this has all been going on ever since then. \n\nI know that's a really long time, and I'm so sorry, I feel like I probably should have moved past it by now, especially given my role at the church. I really have tried to just put it behind me and be strong for everyone else, I promise. I hope it's okay that I'm still struggling with it after all this time... am I answering your question alright?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain places, people, or activities because they remind you of your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess I do, a little bit. Just so I don't have to pass by the community center... or that basketball court. It's just a small detour, really, it doesn't take that much extra time. \n\nAnd, um, if people at church or in the neighborhood start talking about that night... I usually try to gently change the subject, or I just remember something I have to go do in the office and excuse myself. I don't want to be rude to anyone, I just... I get kind of overwhelmed. Is that a normal thing to do? I hope I don't sound like I'm just running away from my problems.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sleeping at night?\n\nPatient: Um... no, actually, I sleep pretty well. I mean, I try to make sure I keep myself really busy during the day—just helping out the congregation and doing all my duties—so by the time I get to bed, I'm usually exhausted enough to just sleep right through. \n\nI hope that's okay? I know you're probably trying to figure out a specific pattern, and I really don't want to mess up your evaluation or be difficult by not having that problem. But yeah, my sleep is mostly fine, thankfully. Does... does that help?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel on edge or easily irritated?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try hard to be patient and understanding with everyone. That's a big part of my calling, you know? Being a source of peace for the congregation. But... I guess, lately, I have been a little... on edge. \n\nI'm so embarrassed to even admit this, but... there have been a few times in ministry meetings where I've just... snapped. Over really small things. I hate how fast I get angry like that, and I always make sure to apologize. I know I shouldn't be acting that way, especially as a pastor. I hope you don't think I'm a bad person for it... I'm really trying to work on it. Is it normal to feel so easily frustrated like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're reliving the moment of your cousin's death, like you're right back there?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I do. I mean, that's actually the main reason I finally decided to come in and talk to someone. I... I didn't want to make a big deal out of it, because I know people go through worse, but... \n\nIt's just that, out of nowhere, I'll get hit with the whole scene again. Like... like I'm right back there. I can see my cousin on the pavement, and... I can hear all the shouting, and see the flashing lights. And the hardest part is, it happens even when I'm just standing in the church office, trying to act completely normal and be there for everyone else. \n\nI try so hard to just push it down and focus on my work, but it feels so real. I feel awful even bringing it up, because I should be stronger than this, right? I'm supposed to be the one helping people through their grief, not... falling apart in the middle of the day. I hope that makes sense? I just want to know if there's a way to make it stop, so I can get back to being the pastor my congregation needs.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Um... I know I shouldn't. I really try not to. As a pastor, I'm always teaching my congregation about grace and trusting in God, so I feel terrible even admitting this to you. I don't want you to think I don't have faith, or that I'm not trying my best to cope, but... yes. I do. \n\nI just... I keep telling myself that if I had only ended the church event a little earlier... or if I had just taken the time to walk him out... or even if I had just kept a better watch over those boys who were hanging around... my cousin would still be here. \n\nI know it's probably not right for me to take all that on, and I'm sorry if it sounds like I'm just wallowing in it. I know I shouldn't dwell on the \"what ifs.\" I really am trying to be strong and move past it. Do you... do you think it's normal to blame yourself like that? I just hope I'm not making your evaluation harder by being so stuck on this.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body watching yourself when these memories of your cousin's death come back?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think I've ever felt quite like that. I hope that's okay—I mean, I want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need. But when it happens, it's really just... I'm right back in my own shoes, feeling everything all over again. \n\nI don't really feel disconnected or like I'm floating above it or watching it happen to someone else. I'm just... trapped right there in the middle of it. I'm sorry if that's not the typical response, or if I'm not explaining it right. Is it... is it normal that I don't have that happen to me? I really just want to make sure I'm being helpful and answering your questions the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid talking or thinking about your feelings related to your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to just focus on the positive, you know? I want to be a good example for the congregation, so I... I try not to dwell on the negative feelings too much. I hope that doesn't make it sound like I'm in denial or anything. I just... I want to handle it the right way.\n\nBut, um... yeah, I guess I do avoid it. I actually... I take the long way around town now, just so I don't have to drive past the community center or the basketball court where it happened. And... if someone brings up that night, even if they mean well, I usually try to change the subject or... or I'll just find an excuse to leave the room. \n\nI know I should probably be more open to talking about it, especially since I encourage others to share their burdens. I feel a bit hypocritical, honestly. I'm sorry, I know that's probably not the healthiest way to deal with things. Is it... is it bad that I do that? I just want to make sure I'm doing what I need to do to get better.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel numb or disconnected from the world around you when you're not having one of these flashbacks?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to stay engaged, you know? I want to be present for my congregation. They rely on me so much, and I always try to put on a brave face for them. I hope you don't think I'm ignoring my duties or anything like that. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest... yes, sometimes I do feel a bit... disconnected. It's hard to explain without sounding like I'm ungrateful for my community. Even when I'm surrounded by people at church, and they're calling me 'brother' and asking me to pray with them... I just feel like... like I'm behind glass. Like they're right there, but nobody can really reach me. \n\nI feel so guilty about it, because I know they love me and they mean well. I shouldn't feel so distant from them. I'm sorry, I know a pastor shouldn't feel that way about his flock. Do you... do you think that makes me a bad leader? I just want to make sure I'm not failing them by feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to keep up with everything. I don't want to let anyone down, so I still show up and do what's expected of me. I hope you know I'm still trying my best... I don't want to sound lazy or ungrateful for the life and the calling God has given me. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest... yeah. It's... it's been really hard lately. I used to just absolutely love preaching, and playing the keys during worship. I used to take pictures all the time, too, and I really loved running with the guys on the basketball court... but now... \n\nNow I mostly just go through the motions with the church stuff, or I just avoid the other things altogether. It's like... none of it really feels alive in me anymore. I feel so awful admitting that out loud to you. I mean, preaching is my life's work, I shouldn't feel empty while I'm doing it. I'm sorry, I know that probably sounds terrible, like I'm losing my faith or something. Do you... is it normal to lose your passion like that? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers so I can get back to being the person I'm supposed to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant problems in your relationships with others or your ability to perform your job as a pastor since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to maintain good relationships, you know? I want everyone at the church to feel supported and loved. I don't want you to think I'm just abandoning my responsibilities or fighting with people on purpose. I really do try to keep the peace.\n\nBut, um... things have been a little rocky lately. I... I actually was put on temporary leave from ministry. It wasn't... I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing so I can heal and get back to serving them the way I should.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think that you're a bad person or that you're worthless because of what happened to your cousin?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try not to use words like 'worthless' to describe myself. I teach my congregation that we all have God-given value, and I don't want you to think I'm just throwing a pity party or ignoring my faith. I just want to make sure I'm not failing them by feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to keep up with everything. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, I really try to process things the right way, you know? I don't want you to think I'm just running away from my feelings or that I'm in denial. I know as a pastor I'm supposed to face grief head-on and trust God with the pain. 

But, um... honestly, it's just so overwhelming sometimes. I do find myself... well, I guess you could call it avoiding things. Like, I'll take the long way around town just so I don't have to drive past the community center or that basketball court. Seeing it just brings up too much. And... if someone brings up that night, even if they mean well, I just... I try to shut the conversation down, or I'll just find an excuse to leave the room. 

I feel terrible doing that, especially when people are just trying to check on me or offer condolences. I know it probably seems rude, and I really don't want to be that kind of person. I'm sorry, I hope you don't think I'm weak for not being able to handle it better. Does that... does that answer what you were asking? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me today, I really appreciate your time. \n\nUm... I hope it's okay to just jump right in, but I guess what brings me in is that I keep getting hit out of nowhere with... well, with the scene of my cousin on that pavement. I'm sorry, I know that sounds a bit dramatic, and I really don't mean to make a huge deal out of it. It's just... it's like I can hear all the shouting and see the lights all over again. \n\nAnd it happens at the worst times, like even when I'm just standing in the church office trying to act normal and do my job. I really do try to keep it together and not let it show, I promise. Does... does that make sense? That's when he was... when he was killed. And I guess this has all been going on ever since then. \n\nI know that's a really long time, and I'm so sorry, I feel like I probably should have moved past it by now, especially given my role at the church. I really have tried to just put it behind me and be strong for everyone else, I promise. I'm really trying to work on it. Is it normal to feel so easily frustrated like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're reliving the moment of your cousin's death, like you're right back there?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I do. I mean, that's actually the main reason I finally decided to come in and talk to someone. I... I didn't want to make a big deal out of it, because I know people go through worse, but... \n\nIt's just that, out of nowhere, I'll get hit with the whole scene again. Like... like I'm right back there. I can see my cousin on the pavement, and... I can hear all the shouting, and see the flashing lights. And the hardest part is, it happens even when I'm just standing in the church office, trying to act completely normal and be there for everyone else. \n\nI try so hard to just push it down and focus on my work, but it feels so real. I feel awful even bringing it up, because I should be stronger than this, right? I'm supposed to be the one helping people through their grief, not... falling apart in the middle of the day. I hope that makes sense? I just want to know if there's a way to make it stop, so I can get back to being the pastor my congregation needs.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Um... I know I shouldn't. I really try not to. As a pastor, I'm always teaching my congregation about grace and trusting in God, so I feel terrible even admitting this to you. I don't want you to think I don't have faith, or that I'm not trying my best to cope, but... yes. I do. \n\nI just... I keep telling myself that if I had only ended the church event a little earlier... or if I had just taken the time to walk him out... or even if I had just kept a better watch over those boys who were hanging around... my cousin would still be here. \n\nI know it's probably not right for me to take all that on, and I'm sorry if it sounds like I'm just wallowing in it. I know I shouldn't dwell on the \"what ifs.\" I really am trying to be strong and move past it. Do you... do you think it's normal to blame yourself like that? I just hope I'm not making your evaluation harder by being so stuck on this.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body watching yourself when these memories of your cousin's death come back?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think I've ever felt quite like that. I hope that's okay—I mean, I want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need. But when it happens, it's really just... I'm right back in my own shoes, feeling everything all over again. \n\nI don't really feel disconnected or like I'm floating above it or watching it happen to someone else. I'm just... trapped right there in the middle of it. I'm sorry if that's not the typical response, or if I'm not explaining it right. Is it... is it normal that I don't have that happen to me? I really just want to make sure I'm being helpful and answering your questions the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid talking or thinking about your feelings related to your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to just focus on the positive, you know? I want to be a good example for the congregation, so I... I try not to dwell on the negative feelings too much. I hope that doesn't make it sound like I'm in denial or anything. I just... I want to handle it the right way.\n\nBut, um... yeah, I guess I do avoid it. I actually... I take the long way around town now, just so I don't have to drive past the community center or the basketball court where it happened. And... if someone brings up that night, even if they mean well, I usually try to change the subject or... or I'll just find an excuse to leave the room. \n\nI know I should probably be more open to talking about it, especially since I encourage others to share their burdens. I feel a bit hypocritical, honestly. I'm sorry, I know that's probably not the healthiest way to deal with things. Is it... is it bad that I do that? I just want to make sure I'm doing what I need to do to get better.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel numb or disconnected from the world around you when you're not having one of these flashbacks?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to stay engaged, you know? I want to be present for my congregation. They rely on me so much, and I always try to put on a brave face for them. I hope you don't think I'm ignoring my duties or anything like that. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest... yes, sometimes I do feel a bit... disconnected. It's hard to explain without sounding like I'm ungrateful for my community. Even when I'm surrounded by people at church, and they're calling me 'brother' and asking me to pray with them... I just feel like... like I'm behind glass. Like they're right there, but nobody can really reach me. \n\nI feel so guilty about it, because I know they love me and they mean well. I shouldn't feel so distant from them. I'm sorry, I know a pastor shouldn't feel that way about his flock. Do you... do you think that makes me a bad leader? I just want to make sure I'm not failing them by feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to keep up with everything. I don't want to let anyone down, so I still show up and do what's expected of me. I hope you know I'm still trying my best... I don't want to sound lazy or ungrateful for the life and the calling God has given me. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest... yeah. It's... it's been really hard lately. I used to just absolutely love preaching, and playing the keys during worship. I used to take pictures all the time, too, and I really loved running with the guys on the basketball court... but now... \n\nNow I mostly just go through the motions with the church stuff, or I just avoid the other things altogether. It's like... none of it really feels alive in me anymore. I feel so awful admitting that out loud to you. I mean, preaching is my life's work, I shouldn't feel empty while I'm doing it. I'm sorry, I know that probably sounds terrible, like I'm losing my faith or something. Do you... is it normal to lose your passion like that? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers so I can get back to being the person I'm supposed to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant problems in your relationships with others or your ability to perform your job as a pastor since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to maintain good relationships, you know? I want everyone at the church to feel supported and loved. I don't want you to think I'm just abandoning my responsibilities or fighting with people on purpose. I really do try to keep the peace.\n\nBut, um... things have been a little rocky lately. I... I actually was put on temporary leave from ministry. It wasn't... I mean, I had a bit of an outburst during a church-board meeting. I just got so overwhelmed, and I blew up in a way I never normally would. I feel so ashamed just saying it. And... I've also had to step back from leading the youth events. The parents and the other leaders... they just don't really trust me to stay steady right now, which I completely understand. \n\nI know a pastor is supposed to be a pillar of strength, and I feel like I've really let everyone down by not keeping my emotions in check. I'm so sorry, I know that makes me sound like a terrible leader. Is it... is it okay that I'm taking this time away? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing so I can heal and get back to serving them the way I should.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think that you're a bad person or that you're worthless because of what happened to your cousin?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try not to use words like 'worthless' to describe myself. I keep telling myself that if I had only ended the church event a little earlier... or if I had just taken the time to walk him out... or even if I had just kept a better watch over those boys who were hanging around... my cousin would still be here. \n\nI know it's probably not right for me to take all that on, and I'm sorry if it sounds like I'm just wallowing in it. I know I shouldn't dwell on the \"what ifs.\" I really am trying to be strong and move past it. Do you... do you think it's normal to blame yourself like that? I just hope I'm not making your evaluation harder by being so stuck on this.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body watching yourself when these memories of your cousin's death come back?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think I've ever felt quite like that. I hope that's okay—I mean, I want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need. But when it happens, it's really just... I'm right back in my own shoes, feeling everything all over again. \n\nI don't really feel disconnected or like I'm floating above it or watching it happen to someone else. I'm just... trapped right there in the middle of it. I'm sorry if that's not the typical response, or if I'm not explaining it right. Is it... is it normal that I don't have that happen to me? I really just want to make sure I'm being helpful and answering your questions the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid talking or thinking about your feelings related to your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to just focus on the positive, you know? I want to be a good example for the congregation, so I... I try not to dwell on the negative feelings too much. I hope that doesn't make it sound like I'm in denial or anything. I just... I want to handle it the right way.\n\nBut, um... yeah, I guess I do avoid it. I actually... I take the long way around town now, just so I don't have to drive past the community center or the basketball court where it happened. And... if someone brings up that night, even if they mean well, I usually try to change the subject or... or I'll just find an excuse to leave the room. \n\nI know I should probably be more open to talking about it, especially since I encourage others to share their burdens. I feel a bit hypocritical, honestly. I'm sorry, I know that's probably not the healthiest way to deal with things. Is it... is it bad that I do that? I just want to make sure I'm doing what I need to do to get better.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel numb or disconnected from the world around you when you're not having one of these flashbacks?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to stay engaged, you know? I want to be present for my congregation. They rely on me so much, and I always try to put on a brave face for them. I hope you don't think I'm ignoring my duties or anything like that. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest... yes, sometimes I do feel a bit... disconnected. It's hard to explain without sounding like I'm ungrateful for my community. Even when I'm surrounded by people at church, and they're calling me 'brother' and asking me to pray with them... I just feel like... like I'm behind glass. Like they're right there, but nobody can really reach me. \n\nI feel so guilty about it, because I know they love me and they mean well. I shouldn't feel so distant from them. I'm sorry, I know a pastor shouldn't feel that way about his flock. Do you... do you think that makes me a bad leader? I just want to make sure I'm not failing them by feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to keep up with everything. I don't want to let anyone down, so I still show up and do what's expected of me. I hope you know I'm still trying my best... I don't want to sound lazy or ungrateful for the life and the calling God has given me. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest... yeah. It's... it's been really hard lately. I used to just absolutely love preaching, and playing the keys during worship. I used to take pictures all the time, too, and I really loved running with the guys on the basketball court... but now... \n\nNow I mostly just go through the motions with the church stuff, or I just avoid the other things altogether. It's like... none of it really feels alive in me anymore. I feel so awful admitting that out loud to you. I mean, preaching is my life's work, I shouldn't feel empty while I'm doing it. I'm sorry, I know that probably sounds terrible, like I'm losing my faith or something. Do you... is it normal to lose your passion like that? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers so I can get back to being the person I'm supposed to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant problems in your relationships with others or your ability to perform your job as a pastor since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to maintain good relationships, you know? I want everyone at the church to feel supported and loved. I don't want you to think I'm just abandoning my responsibilities or fighting with people on purpose. I really do try to keep the peace.\n\nBut, um... things have been a little rocky lately. I... I actually was put on temporary leave from ministry. It wasn't... I mean, I had a bit of an outburst during a church-board meeting. I just got so overwhelmed, and I blew up in a way I never normally would. I feel so ashamed just saying it. And... I've also had to step back from leading the youth events. The parents and the other leaders... they just don't really trust me to stay steady right now, which I completely understand. \n\nI know a pastor is supposed to be a pillar of strength, and I feel like I've really let everyone down by not keeping my emotions in check. I'm so sorry, I know that makes me sound like a terrible leader. Is it... is it okay that I'm taking this time away? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing so I can heal and get back to serving them the way I should.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think that you're a bad person or that you're worthless because of what happened to your cousin?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try not to use words like 'worthless' to describe myself. I teach my congregation that we all have God-given value, and I don't want you to think I'm just throwing a pity party or ignoring my faith. I really do try to keep a healthy perspective on things.\n\nBut... if I'm being honest... it's just so hard not to feel like a terrible person right now. I feel so ashamed to admit this to you, because I know I should trust the Lord, but... I just keep playing that night over and over in my head. I keep telling myself that if I had just ended the event earlier... or if I had walked him out... or just kept better watch over those boys... my cousin would still be here. \n\nI was the one in charge, so it feels like it's all my fault. I'm sorry, I know I shouldn't be so hard on myself, and I hope you don't think less of me for struggling with this guilt. Do you... do you think it's normal to blame yourself like that? I just want to make sure I'm answering you correctly so I can figure out how to get past this.\n\nDoctor: Have you stopped doing things that you used to enjoy since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I know we touched on this a little bit already, and I really want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture. I don't want to sound like a broken record or like I'm just complaining, because I really am trying to push through and do what I'm supposed to do. \n\nBut, um... yeah, like I mentioned before... I really have stopped doing a lot of the things I used to love. It's just... I used to get so much joy out of preaching and playing the keys for worship. And I loved taking pictures, and playing basketball with the guys... but since everything happened, I just... I either just go through the motions because I have to, or I avoid those things completely. \n\nIt just feels like the life has been drained out of them, you know? None of it feels alive in me anymore. I feel so bad repeating that, because I know I should be stronger in my faith and find joy in the Lord's work. I'm sorry if I sound ungrateful or like I'm not trying hard enough. Is it... is it helpful for me to explain it that way? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a daze or spaced out when you're not having one of these flashbacks?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to stay focused, you know? I want to make sure I'm doing my job well, even with everything going on. I don't want you to think I'm just zoning out on purpose or ignoring my duties. I really do try to stay present and engaged with my work.\n\nBut, um... lately, it has been a little difficult to concentrate. It's not like I'm completely out of it all the time, but... when I sit down to write a sermon, or even just answer church emails, my mind just kind of... blanks out. I'll find myself reading the same line five or six times, and I just can't seem to take in what it says. It's like my brain just won't process the words. \n\nI feel so bad admitting that, because I know people are relying on me to be sharp and to communicate clearly. I'm sorry, I know that probably makes me sound really scattered and unprofessional. Is it... does that make sense, the way I described it? I mean, I really try not to let it affect my daily routine too much, because people depend on me, you know? But... well, I do tend to take the long way around town now. Just so I don't have to pass by the community center... or that basketball court. It's just a small detour, really, it doesn't take that much extra time. \n\nAnd, um, if people at church or in the neighborhood start talking about that night... I usually try to gently change the subject, or I just remember something I have to go do in the office and excuse myself. I don't want to be rude to anyone, I just... I get kind of overwhelmed. Is that a normal thing to do? I hope I don't sound like I'm just running away from my problems.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sleeping at night?\n\nPatient: Um... no, actually, I sleep pretty well. I mean, I try to make sure I keep myself really busy during the day—just helping out the congregation and doing all my duties—so by the time I get to bed, I'm usually exhausted enough to just sleep right through. \n\nI hope that's okay? I know you're probably trying to figure out a specific pattern, and I really don't want to mess up your evaluation or be difficult by not having that problem. But yeah, my sleep is mostly fine, thankfully. Does... does that help?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel on edge or easily irritated?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try hard to be patient and understanding with everyone. That's a big part of my calling, you know? Being a source of peace for the congregation. But... I guess, lately, I have been a little... on edge. \n\nI'm so embarrassed to even admit this, but... there have been a few times in ministry meetings where I've just... snapped. Over really small things. I actually raised my voice at some of the deacons, and even the youth leaders, almost like... like they were against me or something. \n\nI always feel terrible right after, I really do. I hate how fast I get angry like that, and I always make sure to apologize. I know I shouldn't be acting that way, especially as a pastor. I hope you don't think I'm a bad person for it... I'm really trying to work on it. Is it normal to feel so easily frustrated like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're reliving the moment of your cousin's death, like you're right back there?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I do. I mean, that's actually the main reason I finally decided to come in and talk to someone. I... I didn't want to make a big deal out of it, because I know people go through worse, but... \n\nIt's just that, out of nowhere, I'll get hit with the whole scene again. Like... like I'm right back there. I can see my cousin on the pavement, and... I can hear all the shouting, and see the flashing lights. And the hardest part is, it happens even when I'm just standing in the church office, trying to act completely normal and be there for everyone else. \n\nI try so hard to just push it down and focus on my work, but it feels so real. I feel awful even bringing it up, because I should be stronger than this, right? I'm supposed to be the one helping people through their grief, not... falling apart in the middle of the day. I hope that makes sense? I just want to know if there's a way to make it stop, so I can get back to being the pastor my congregation needs.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Um... I know I shouldn't. I really try not to. As a pastor, I'm always teaching my congregation about grace and trusting in God, so I feel terrible even admitting this to you. I don't want you to think I don't have faith, or that I'm not trying my best to cope, but... yes. I do. \n\nI just... I keep telling myself that if I had only ended the church event a little earlier... or if I had just taken the time to walk him out... or even if I had just kept a better watch over those boys who were hanging around... my cousin would still be here. \n\nI know it's probably not right for me to take all that on, and I'm sorry if it sounds like I'm just wallowing in it. I know I shouldn't dwell on the \"what ifs.\" I really am trying to be strong and move past it. Do you... do you think it's normal to blame yourself like that? I just hope I'm not making your evaluation harder by being so stuck on this.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body watching yourself when these memories of your cousin's death come back?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think I've ever felt quite like that. I hope that's okay—I mean, I want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need. But when it happens, it's really just... I'm right back in my own shoes, feeling everything all over again. \n\nI don't really feel disconnected or like I'm floating above it or watching it happen to someone else. I'm just... trapped right there in the middle of it. I'm sorry if that's not the typical response, or if I'm not explaining it right. Is it... is it normal that I don't have that happen to me? I really just want to make sure I'm being helpful and answering your questions the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid talking or thinking about your feelings related to your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to just focus on the positive, you know? I want to be a good example for the congregation, so I... I try not to dwell on the negative feelings too much. I hope that doesn't make it sound like I'm in denial or anything. I just... I want to handle it the right way.\n\nBut, um... yeah, I guess I do avoid it. I actually... I take the long way around town now, just so I don't have to drive past the community center or the basketball court where it happened. And... if someone brings up that night, even if they mean well, I usually try to change the subject or... or I'll just find an excuse to leave the room. \n\nI know I should probably be more open to talking about it, especially since I encourage others to share their burdens. I feel a bit hypocritical, honestly. I'm sorry, I know that's probably not the healthiest way to deal with things. Is it... is it bad that I do that? I just want to make sure I'm doing what I need to do to get better.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel numb or disconnected from the world around you when you're not having one of these flashbacks?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to stay engaged, you know? I want to be present for my congregation. They rely on me so much, and I always try to put on a brave face for them. I hope you don't think I'm ignoring my duties or anything like that. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest... yes, sometimes I do feel a bit... disconnected. It's hard to explain without sounding like I'm ungrateful for my community. Even when I'm surrounded by people at church, and they're calling me 'brother' and asking me to pray with them... I just feel like... like I'm behind glass. Like they're right there, but nobody can really reach me. \n\nI feel so guilty about it, because I know they love me and they mean well. I shouldn't feel so distant from them. I'm sorry, I know a pastor shouldn't feel that way about his flock. Do you... do you think that makes me a bad leader? I just want to make sure I'm not failing them by feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to keep up with everything. I don't want to let anyone down, so I still show up and do what's expected of me. I hope you know I'm still trying my best... I don't want to sound lazy or ungrateful for the life and the calling God has given me. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest... yeah. It's... it's been really hard lately. I used to just absolutely love preaching, and playing the keys during worship. I used to take pictures all the time, too, and I really loved running with the guys on the basketball court... but now... \n\nNow I mostly just go through the motions with the church stuff, or I just avoid the other things altogether. It's like... none of it really feels alive in me anymore. I feel so awful admitting that out loud to you. I mean, preaching is my life's work, I shouldn't feel empty while I'm doing it. I'm sorry, I know that probably sounds terrible, like I'm losing my faith or something. Do you... is it normal to lose your passion like that? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers so I can get back to being the person I'm supposed to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant problems in your relationships with others or your ability to perform your job as a pastor since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to maintain good relationships, you know? I want everyone at the church to feel supported and loved. I don't want you to think I'm just abandoning my responsibilities or fighting with people on purpose. I really do try to keep the peace.\n\nBut, um... things have been a little rocky lately. I... I actually was put on temporary leave from ministry. It wasn't... I mean, I had a bit of an outburst during a church-board meeting. I just got so overwhelmed, and I blew up in a way I never normally would. I feel so ashamed just saying it. And... I've also had to step back from leading the youth events. The parents and the other leaders... they just don't really trust me to stay steady right now, which I completely understand. \n\nI know a pastor is supposed to be a pillar of strength, and I feel like I've really let everyone down by not keeping my emotions in check. I'm so sorry, I know that makes me sound like a terrible leader. Is it... is it okay that I'm taking this time away? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing so I can heal and get back to serving them the way I should.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think that you're a bad person or that you're worthless because of what happened to your cousin?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try not to use words like 'worthless' to describe myself. I teach my congregation that we all have God-given value, and I don't want you to think I'm just throwing a pity party or ignoring my faith. I really do try to keep a healthy perspective on things.\n\nBut... if I'm being honest... it's just so hard not to feel like a terrible person right now. I feel so ashamed to admit this to you, because I know I should trust the Lord, but... I just keep playing that night over and over in my head. I keep telling myself that if I had just ended the event earlier... or if I had walked him out... or just kept better watch over those boys... my cousin would still be here. \n\nI was the one in charge, so it feels like it's all my fault. I'm sorry, I know I shouldn't be so hard on myself, and I hope you don't think less of me for struggling with this guilt. Do you... do you think it's normal to blame yourself like that? I just want to make sure I'm answering you correctly so I can figure out how to get past this.\n\nDoctor: Have you stopped doing things that you used to enjoy since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I know we touched on this a little bit already, and I really want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture. I don't want to sound like a broken record or like I'm just complaining, because I really am trying to push through and do what I'm supposed to do. \n\nBut, um... yeah, like I mentioned before... I really have stopped doing a lot of the things I used to love. It's just... I used to get so much joy out of preaching and playing the keys for worship. And I loved taking pictures, and playing basketball with the guys... but since everything happened, I just... I either just go through the motions because I have to, or I avoid those things completely. \n\nIt just feels like the life has been drained out of them, you know? None of it feels alive in me anymore. I feel so bad repeating that, because I know I should be stronger in my faith and find joy in the Lord's work. I'm sorry if I sound ungrateful or like I'm not trying hard enough. Is it... is it helpful for me to explain it that way? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a daze or spaced out when you're not having one of these flashbacks?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to stay focused, you know? I want to make sure I'm doing my job well, even with everything going on. I don't want you to think I'm just zoning out on purpose or ignoring my duties. I really do try to stay present and engaged with my work.\n\nBut, um... lately, it has been a little difficult to concentrate. It's not like I'm completely out of it all the time, but... when I sit down to write a sermon, or even just answer church emails, my mind just kind of... blanks out. I'll find myself reading the same line five or six times, and I just can't seem to take in what it says. It's like my brain just won't process the words. \n\nI feel so bad admitting that, because I know people are relying on me to be sharp and to communicate clearly. I'm sorry, I know that probably makes me sound really scattered and unprofessional. Is it... does that make sense, the way I described it? I just want to make sure I'm answering your question properly so we can figure out what's going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you try to avoid thinking about your emotions related to your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to process things the right way, you know? I hope that's okay—I mean, I want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need. But when it happens, it's really just... I'm right back in my own shoes, feeling everything all over again. \n\nI don't really feel disconnected or like I'm floating above it or watching it happen to someone else. I'm just... trapped right there in the middle of it. I'm sorry if that's not the typical response, or if I'm not explaining it right. Is it... is it normal that I don't have that happen to me? I really just want to make sure I'm being helpful and answering your questions the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid talking or thinking about your feelings related to your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to just focus on the positive, you know? I want to be a good example for the congregation, so I... I try not to dwell on the negative feelings too much. I hope that doesn't make it sound like I'm in denial or anything. I just... I want to handle it the right way.\n\nBut, um... yeah, I guess I do avoid it. I actually... I take the long way around town now, just so I don't have to drive past the community center or the basketball court where it happened. I feel a bit hypocritical, honestly. I'm sorry, I know that's probably not the healthiest way to deal with things. Is it... is it bad that I do that? I just want to make sure I'm doing what I need to do to get better.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel numb or disconnected from the world around you when you're not having one of these flashbacks?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to stay engaged, you know? I want to be present for my congregation. They rely on me so much, and I always try to put on a brave face for them. I hope you don't think I'm ignoring my duties or anything like that. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest... yes, sometimes I do feel a bit... disconnected. It's hard to explain without sounding like I'm ungrateful for my community. Even when I'm surrounded by people at church, and they're calling me 'brother' and asking me to pray with them... I just feel like... like I'm behind glass. Like they're right there, but nobody can really reach me. \n\nI feel so guilty about it, because I know they love me and they mean well. I shouldn't feel so distant from them. I'm sorry, I know a pastor shouldn't feel that way about his flock. Do you... do you think that makes me a bad leader? I just want to make sure I'm not failing them by feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to keep up with everything. I don't want to let anyone down, so I still show up and do what's expected of me. I hope you know I'm still trying my best... I don't want to sound lazy or ungrateful for the life and the calling God has given me. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest... yeah. It's... it's been really hard lately. I used to just absolutely love preaching, and playing the keys during worship. I used to take pictures all the time, too, and I really loved running with the guys on the basketball court... but now... \n\nNow I mostly just go through the motions with the church stuff, or I just avoid the other things altogether. It's like... none of it really feels alive in me anymore. I feel so awful admitting that out loud to you. I mean, preaching is my life's work, I shouldn't feel empty while I'm doing it. I'm sorry, I know that probably sounds terrible, like I'm losing my faith or something. Do you... is it normal to lose your passion like that? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers so I can get back to being the person I'm supposed to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant problems in your relationships with others or your ability to perform your job as a pastor since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to maintain good relationships, you know? I want everyone at the church to feel supported and loved. I don't want you to think I'm just abandoning my responsibilities or fighting with people on purpose. I really do try to keep the peace.\n\nBut, um... things have been a little rocky lately. I... I actually was put on temporary leave from ministry. It wasn't... I mean, I had a bit of an outburst during a church-board meeting. I just got so overwhelmed, and I blew up in a way I never normally would. I feel so ashamed just saying it. And... I've also had to step back from leading the youth events. The parents and the other leaders... they just don't really trust me to stay steady right now, which I completely understand. \n\nI know a pastor is supposed to be a pillar of strength, and I feel like I've really let everyone down by not keeping my emotions in check. I'm so sorry, I know that makes me sound like a terrible leader. Is it... is it okay that I'm taking this time away? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing so I can heal and get back to serving them the way I should.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think that you're a bad person or that you're worthless because of what happened to your cousin?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try not to use words like 'worthless' to describe myself. I teach my congregation that we all have God-given value, and I don't want you to think I'm just throwing a pity party or ignoring my faith. I really do try to keep a healthy perspective on things.\n\nBut... if I'm being honest... it's just so hard not to feel like a terrible person right now. I feel so ashamed to admit this to you, because I know I should trust the Lord, but... I just keep playing that night over and over in my head. I keep telling myself that if I had just ended the event earlier... or if I had walked him out... or just kept better watch over those boys... my cousin would still be here. \n\nI was the one in charge, so it feels like it's all my fault. I'm sorry, I know I shouldn't be so hard on myself, and I hope you don't think less of me for struggling with this guilt. Do you... do you think it's normal to blame yourself like that? I just want to make sure I'm answering you correctly so I can figure out how to get past this.\n\nDoctor: Have you stopped doing things that you used to enjoy since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I know we touched on this a little bit already, and I really want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture. I don't want to sound like a broken record or like I'm just complaining, because I really am trying to push through and do what I'm supposed to do. \n\nBut, um... yeah, like I mentioned before... I really have stopped doing a lot of the things I used to love. It's just... I used to get so much joy out of preaching and playing the keys for worship. And I loved taking pictures, and playing basketball with the guys... but since everything happened, I just... I either just go through the motions because I have to, or I avoid those things completely. \n\nIt just feels like the life has been drained out of them, you know? None of it feels alive in me anymore. I feel so bad repeating that, because I know I should be stronger in my faith and find joy in the Lord's work. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a daze or spaced out when you're not having one of these flashbacks?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to stay focused, you know? I really do try to stay present and engaged with my work.\n\nBut, um... lately, it has been a little difficult to concentrate. It's not like I'm completely out of it all the time, but... when I sit down to write a sermon, or even just answer church emails, my mind just kind of... blanks out. I'll find myself reading the same line five or six times, and I just can't seem to take in what it says. It's like my brain just won't process the words. \n\nI feel so bad admitting that, because I know people are relying on me to be sharp and to communicate clearly. I'm sorry, I know that probably makes me sound really scattered and unprofessional. Is it... does that make sense, the way I described it? I just want to make sure I'm answering your question properly so we can figure out what's going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you try to avoid thinking about your emotions related to your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to process things the right way, you know? Seeing it just brings up too much. And... if someone brings up that night, even if they mean well, I just... I try to shut the conversation down, or I'll just find an excuse to leave the room. \n\nI feel terrible doing that, especially when people are just trying to check on me or offer condolences. I know it probably seems rude, and I really don't want to be that kind of person. I'm sorry, I hope you don't think I'm weak for not being able to handle it better. Does that... does that answer what you were asking? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to find very meaningful or enjoyable, but now feel completely uninterested in?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I know we kind of touched on this earlier, and I really don't want to sound like I'm just repeating myself or, you know, complaining too much. I really am trying my best to stay engaged and do the things I'm supposed to do. I don't want you to think I've just given up or anything.\n\nBut, um... yeah, like I was saying before... it's just been really hard to find that spark again. I used to absolutely love preaching, and playing the keys during worship was just... it was everything to me. And outside of church, I loved taking pictures, and just getting out and running with the guys on the basketball court. \n\nBut now... I don't know, I just mostly go through the motions. Or I just end up avoiding those things altogether. It's like... none of it feels alive in me anymore, you know? It's just flat. I feel so guilty saying that, especially about ministry, because I know I should be finding joy in serving. I'm really sorry if it sounds like I'm not trying hard enough to push through it. Is it okay that I explained it that way again? I just want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture of what's going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think that you're a failure as a pastor because of your cousin's death and your reaction to it?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to hold onto my faith, you know? Like I completely failed God, and my family, and... and the kids who trusted me. \n\nI feel so ashamed admitting that out loud to you. Does that... did I answer your question okay?
